# 文档覆盖

来源为 [Solid next documentation/solid-2.0](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/tree/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0)。当前全文索引 3070 个原始源单元，分类为 2107 个行为声明、963 个导航、解释或未来设计单元。代码块完整原文索引见 fenced-examples.json；注释与独立代码合同保留自己的稳定 locator，不能以代码块数量替代行为分母。

**covered / total：1884 / 2107**；另有 13 个不可测试声明和 210 个可测试但尚缺断言的声明。covered 表示已有检查合同的断言，也包括 findings 中已缩减的红例；不表示框架全通过。一个 compound statement 的全部保证都得到证明才记为 covered。重复合同保留各自 source locator，可引用同一测试。

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L801:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L801) | The channel is created by whichever copy touches the key first, and its capabilities are that copy's — the packages version together, and with two copies at different versions the behaviour falls back to the older one's (a copy from before the `bodies` option, reached first, knows no bodies set and takes bodies for every `"call"` listener — and for every `"request"` listener, since its `observed(type, "bodies")` ignores the facet). | 这段兼容性声明没有指定 before-bodies/before-request 版本或 commit；HEAD 与 rc.13 均具备这些能力，无法给未指定旧版选取唯一可复验基线。当前两副本共享和新 record 的公开行为另测。 |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L801:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L801) | The emitters are the runtimes', so a listener for a record the loaded runtime predates hears nothing: the channel is generic and takes a `"request"` subscription from any copy, but only a `@solidjs/web` from after the record emits one — a newer `solid-js` beside an older `@solidjs/web` gives a `"request"` listener silence, not an error. | 这段兼容性声明没有指定 before-bodies/before-request 版本或 commit；HEAD 与 rc.13 均具备这些能力，无法给未指定旧版选取唯一可复验基线。当前两副本共享和新 record 的公开行为另测。 |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L801:8](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L801) | Delivery allocates nothing — the channel keeps one listener array per type, replaced (never mutated) on subscribe and unsubscribe, so an emit in progress finishes over the array it started with and a listener unsubscribing mid-delivery neither skips nor double-calls anyone that round. | 精确零分配/编译后每 hook 零成本/宿主时钟量化是架构或宿主实现声明；有限公共运行时样本不能证明其全称成本承诺。已测可见delivery顺序与prod inert部分，不能把它当整体通过。 |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L942:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L942) | \| `INVARIANT_VIOLATION`              \| error      \| error          \| Internal consistency check failed (throws under `__TEST__`, reported in dev)                                                                                      \| | 内部状态一致性诊断，合法公共API无法刻意触发；需破坏私有图结构。 |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L727:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L727) | `data.reason` says which: `"spread"` (`error`, throws) — the slot's whole return spread onto an element, the retired shape (the client would decide what it owns and the template could not show it); `"stringified"` (`warn`) — the stand-in was coerced to a string (a template literal, a concatenation, `String()`), so it is not the whole value; `"coerced"` (`warn`) — the stand-in was used in an expression (a comparison, arithmetic, `==`; `Symbol.toPrimitive` with a `number`/`default` hint), which the server cannot evaluate because it does not have the value — the decision belongs in the client fill, which returns the decided value; `"inline"` (`warn`) — it reached `ssrClassName`/`ssrStyle` inside template quotes, which happens only when the element was compiled without the `serverComponents` compiler option (the option makes a dynamic `class`/`style` a whole-attribute hole); `"markup"` (`warn`) — read off a slot whose client fill returned content, not an object (a slot renders markup **or** data); `"server-local"` (`warn`) — a `ref`/`on*` position on a server intrinsic received a plain server function, which can never run; `"tuple"` (`warn`) — an `on*` position received an array (`onKeyDown={[row.key, 1]}`): a marker names keys, never data, so nothing binds — return the tuple from the fill instead, where the client binds it as client JSX does (only a `ref` position takes an array, whose refs merge); `"reserved-key"` (`warn`, document face) — the fill's object used a key the slot's range occupies (beginning with `$` or a digit, `length`, `slice`, `t`/`h`/`p`, `then`, `constructor`/`toString`/`valueOf`/`toJSON` — the explicit set; every other key, `filter` or `map` included, is a property read), so a read of it is the range, not a value; `"arg"` (`warn`) — a stand-in was passed in another slot call's argument (`props.child({ parentId: parent.id })`, or nested in plain objects and arrays: `{ nested: { x: row.done } }`, `[row.done]` — a `Map`, `Set` or class instance is not walked), data the server does not have; the arg carries `undefined` at that path on both faces — the record and the document face's t=0 fill (`data.from`/`data.fromKey` name the stand-in, `data.path` the position inside the arg when nested; a stand-in reachable at two paths is reported once, at the first); `"prop"` (`warn`) — a stand-in at a `prop:*` key of a runtime spread, a property position the server cannot bind (the compiled form drops `prop:*` as SSR always has, silently); `"fill-shape"` (`warn`, kind `render`, client) — the client side has the wrong shape: the fill returned something other than a plain object (`null`, an array, a DOM node, an async value, a primitive — `data.shape`), or the prop the markup reads as data is not a function; nothing binds until it is; `"text-shape"` (`warn`, kind `render`, client) — a text position's value is not a string, number, nullish or boolean (an object, an array, a DOM node, a function, an async value — `data.shape`, with `data.key`): a text position renders a primitive as a client insert does, markup belongs in a template slot, and the text clears; `"orphan"` (`warn`, kind `render` — from the frame client's slot sync) — an element carries `_s:*` markers for an occurrence whose positions can never bind: `data.why` is `"fill"` when no client fill resolves for the prop (the prop was not passed, or its name differs between the sides), or `"record"` when a **called** occurrence (`prop#n`) has no args record once records can no longer arrive (the producer emits the record ahead of the markup that reads it, so a missing one is the protocol out of step — a client and server from different builds, or a runtime id mismatch — never a fill mistake; a bare occurrence named by the prop alone has no record by design and never reports); `data.elements` are the marked elements, reported once per occurrence per frame. | 全部 reason 与 client shape/key/occurrence/why/elements 已精确断言；reserved-key 全集合、arg 多路径 first-only 与双面 undefined 尚缺，不能整句 covered。 |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1127:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1127) | **Excluding the observer.** `OBSERVE.exclude(owner)` marks an owner subtree as the observer's own: diagnostics whose subject sits under it are built (a throwing site still throws) but never delivered or printed, and the attribution engine records no run for its computations, charges none of them to an interaction, counts no write to its signals or stores toward an interaction, and does not spend a once-per-key slot (`IMMUTABLE_UPDATE_IN_STORE`'s per-path memory) on them. | 本句完整合同尚需补充独立断言。 |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1131:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1131) | The fields the level governs: `ChangeRecord.prev`/`value` and `HeldWrite.prev`/`value` — previews of the written values (`preview()`: strings quoted and cut at 40 characters, numbers/booleans verbatim, everything else a type tag such as `Array(12)` or `[Object]`); `ChangeOrigin.target` (and so `InteractionEvent.target`, `HoldEvent.interaction.target`) — the element hit as the runtime described it, `tag#id "text"` with up to 30 characters of `textContent` for anything that is not an `input`/`textarea`/`select`, so a label but also whatever a `<td>` said; and every sentence the engine builds from those — `formatRerun`, `formatOrigin`, the `SILENT_HOLD`/`LONG_HOLD` verdicts (`click on button#next "Next →" wrote "page" (1 → 2)…`, with `data.interaction.target` structured beside), `OPTIMISTIC_REVERTED` (which quotes the shown and settled values, `data.shown`/`data.truth`). | 本句完整合同尚需补充独立断言。 |

(Making the loader wait itself part of the transition, so the location write is the one to wrap, is router work — planned for the Solid 2 TanStack adapter, not yet done.) Such a router also loses the click: the interaction frame is gone by the time it publishes. | 本句完整合同尚需补充独立断言。 |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1179:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1179) | `history("interaction")` keeps one `InteractionEvent` per dispatch instead (delivered on `OBSERVE.records.subscribe("interaction", …)` as it settles), with a start, an end, and the pieces attached, so a consumer building a span per interaction (an APM adapter) neither infers the end from an idle gap nor sums quantized per-run times to approximate the wall clock: | 本句完整合同尚需补充独立断言。 |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1194:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1194) | - `sources`: per set of async sources waited on, `holds`, `heldMs`/`worstMs`, the `silent` subset (no acknowledgement at any duration) with its `silentMs`, `latestOnly` (the only acknowledgement was a `latest()` shadow — the input showed, nothing said "loading"), `long`/`longMs` (holds whose tail reached `longHolds.infoMs`, acknowledged or not — the `LONG_HOLD` shape; `longMs` sums the tails), `acknowledgedBy` ranked by affordance (`isPending:`, `latest:`, `optimistic:`, `affects:` prefixed with the node), the `interactions` and root `writes` that were held, and how many holds an `action` opened or joined. | 本句完整合同尚需补充独立断言。 |

| [10-server-functions:L228:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L228) | - **Nested sources are bounded; the standing answer is the top-level one.** The persistent thing is the live source itself — the iterable the function returns, or the component it returns. | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |
| [10-server-functions:L228:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L228) | The promises and iterables nested in a value answer are not assumed persistent: they are bounded traces that end on their own, which is what lets the loop count them (a completion is every one of them done) and what lets a document render carry them — its serialization pumps each to its end, as it does any streamed answer; a standing stream nested in a value would hold the document open, and is misuse the frames' safety cap and diagnostic cover. | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |
| [10-server-functions:L228:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L228) | What a nested source needs under SSR is not a handoff but **sharing**: a generator yields to one reader, and the serializer pumping the answer to the client and a memo reading `answer().progress` are two — so every read the runtime makes on the server goes through a seat on a shared multicast of the source (`shareAsyncIterable`; the serializer takes its seat through the border walk at `context.serialize`), and both see the whole sequence off one pump. | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |
| [10-server-functions:L229:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L229) | - **Undeclared death is an error.** An undeclared streaming answer that dies mid-stream rejects the consumer's pull like any transport failure — `<Errored>` catches it, nothing resumes. | Declared reconnect failure 014 does not exercise the undeclared rejection path. |
| [10-server-functions:L230:10](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L230) | A buffering proxy that ignores `X-Accel-Buffering` is silent until the stream dies. | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |
| [10-server-functions:L230:12](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L230) | Nothing an app wrote against today's `live` changes: a live call _is_ a direct call, so every client and server hook applies as it does to one. | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |
| [10-server-functions:L230:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L230) | It is the same connection with the same timeouts as today's streamed answer; what differs is what is listed here — the content type and two headers, the event envelope, the heartbeat, `Last-Event-ID` on reconnect — and the content type is one middleboxes are trained to pass through unbuffered and uncompressed (the reason SvelteKit moved `query.live` off ndjson). | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |
| [10-server-functions:L230:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L230) | A third caller kind receiving a third shape gets a third path, for the reason the data address exists (#3094): shared caches key on the URL, not on request headers nothing tells them to vary by, and a read carries no header of the transport's own (#3406). | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |
| [10-server-functions:L230:8](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L230) | The precondition is a backend that holds connections, over HTTP/2 (six connections per origin under HTTP/1.1; the Vite dev server speaks HTTP/2 with `server.https`, and dev warns past five live connections without it) — the precondition LiveView, Datastar, and `query.live` all share, and there is nothing to configure. | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |
| [10-server-functions:L230:9](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L230) | A platform that kills a response at a ceiling kills each live response there; the loop reads it as a death, reconnects with its position, and `onstatus` shows the cycle — `query.live`'s behavior on the same platform. | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |
| [10-server-functions:L249:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L249) | The client's binding reconnects on death through `live`'s loop; the document render takes first values from the component's sources and hands off (the brand on the component marks the frame's scope); hydration adopts the document's markup and the loop reconnects from there. | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |
| [10-server-functions:L251:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L251) | **Routers.** A data layer may apply `live` (and `GET`) on the author's behalf — a live query is "`live` + shared" — but the core spelling is the documented one, and the reliable site for `GET` is the definition: the has-method grant arms when the module that applies `GET` is loaded on the server, and the function's own module always is. | Executable coverage for this precise behavior is not yet implemented. |

| [01-reactivity-batching-effects:L7:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/01-reactivity-batching-effects.md#L7) | Effects are split into a tracking phase and an effect phase, enabling safer async and resumability. | 01/effects-order; 01/effect-prev-cleanup |
| [01-reactivity-batching-effects:L12:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/01-reactivity-batching-effects.md#L12) | - **Strict top-level access (new):** Top-level reactive reads in component body can accidentally capture dependencies and re-run in async situations; in 2.0 we warn in dev unless the read is inside createMemo/createEffect or explicit `untrack`. | 08/diagnostic-reactive_write_in_owned_scope; 08/diagnostic-strict_read_untracked; 01/microtask |
| [01-reactivity-batching-effects:L13:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/01-reactivity-batching-effects.md#L13) | - **Batching:** Synchronous batching with `batch()` is replaced by default microtask batching. | 01/microtask; 01/flush-reads |
| [01-reactivity-batching-effects:L13:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/01-reactivity-batching-effects.md#L13) | Setters queue work; reads (and DOM/effects) reflect the update after the batch flushes (next microtask, or via `flush()`). | 01/microtask; 01/flush-reads |

| [04-stores:L132:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/04-stores.md#L132) | A late write is applied to the store and reaches subscribers at the next flush, which it schedules itself when no async run is in flight to do so. | 04/projection-late-write |
| [04-stores:L132:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/04-stores.md#L132) | Writes through a superseded or disposed draft are dropped silently — a callback from a previous run cannot write into the state the current run owns, and a leaked callback after unmount is a no-op — but the cleanup is still yours (`onCleanup(unsubscribe)` above); the draft going dead does not stop the subscription. | 04/stale-draft |
| [04-stores:L132:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/04-stores.md#L132) | One carve-out: with `seedLoadingValue: true`, the first run works a detached copy of the seed rather than the live draft (the seed *is* the displayed value during the loading window), so a callback captured during that first run stops writing through once it lands — set up long-lived subscriptions on later runs, or without `seedLoadingValue`. | 04/seed-loading-detached-draft |
| [04-stores:L134:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/04-stores.md#L134) | Reading a not-yet-settled async source inside a derive throws; that propagation is load-bearing. | 04/projection-async-propagation-swallowed; 04/projection-async-propagation-escaped |

| [04-stores:L134:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/04-stores.md#L134) | A derive that wraps its reads in a broad `try/catch` "completes" with partial state and severs that channel — the browser build masks the mistake (dependencies register before the throw, so reactivity re-runs the derive anyway), but the node build freezes at the first result. | 04/projection-async-propagation-swallowed; 04/projection-async-propagation-escaped |

(Reference keying remains fine when your data flow reuses objects for unchanged records.) | 04/shallow-for-keyed-replacement |

| [06-actions-optimistic:L158:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L158) | Yield it from an action to hold the transaction — and every optimistic override riding it — open until the world confirms: | 06/until-truthy; 06/until-authority; 06/until-structural-authority |
| [06-actions-optimistic:C165:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L165) | setMessages((m) => { m.push({ clientId, text, pending: true }); }); // optimistic | 06/until-authority |
| [06-actions-optimistic:C167:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L167) | // Hold until the live source echoes the write: | 06/until-authority |
| [06-actions-optimistic:C169:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L169) | // settle → the override drops onto data that already contains the real row | 06/until-authority |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L175:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L175) | - **Authoritative-view reads.** Inside an action, the predicate reads the *authoritative* view, and the carve-out is exactly one layer deep: active optimistic **overrides** (and the structure they add — membership, array length, `Object.keys`) are invisible to it, so your own tentative write can never satisfy your own ack — even on the single-primitive shape above, where the optimistic store *is* the live-fed store. | 06/until-structural-authority |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L175:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L175) | Everything else reads normally, **including uncommitted transition-staged data**: truth that arrives *into* the open transaction (the landing of a `refresh()` the action issued, an entangled write) stages and cannot commit until the hold releases — a predicate that refused staged reads would deadlock on the very data it is waiting for. | 06/until-staged-source |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L176:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L176) | - **Level-triggered, transport-agnostic.** The predicate is a condition over state, not a listener on a channel. | 06/until-level-triggered |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L176:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L176) | If the confirmation landed before you started waiting, the predicate is already true — there is no subscribe/deliver race, no message buffering, and no coupling to how truth arrives (push, refetch, another tab). | 06/until-level-triggered |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L178:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L178) | Failure composes with action semantics. | 06/until-predicate-error; 06/until-async-error; 06/until-timeout; 06/until-abort; 06/action-rejection; 06/overlay-signal-failure |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L178:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L178) | `until` rejects on a predicate error, an async source rejection, `{ timeout }` (with `TimeoutError`), or `{ signal }` abort (with the signal's reason); the rejection is thrown back into the generator at the `yield` point — catchable there, or the action fails and its optimistic state reverts. | 06/until-predicate-error; 06/until-async-error; 06/until-timeout; 06/until-abort; 06/action-rejection; 06/overlay-signal-failure |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L186:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L186) | Where `resolve(fn)` answers "what is this value" (first settled value, whatever it is), `until(fn)` answers "when does the world confirm this condition" (first *truthy* settle), and an awaited `refresh(x)` answers "when has this re-ask settled" (no condition at all — the settle point of a refetch you issued). | 05/promise-resolution; 06/until-truthy; 05/refresh-delivery |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L186:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L186) | They share delivery machinery; the read-semantics differences are deliberate: `resolve` reads your own transaction's view, overrides included — it is reporting your state. | 06/resolve-own-overlay |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L186:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L186) | `until` reads the authoritative view, overrides carved out — it is waiting on the world, and your own optimism must not be able to answer for it. | 06/until-authority |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L186:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L186) | `refresh` delivers the landed (staged-or-committed) value, never the caller's override. | findings/022-refresh-optimistic-authority/repro.test.ts |
| [06-actions-optimistic:L186:5](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/06-actions-optimistic.md#L186) | All see transition-staged data; none can deadlock on a value the surrounding action holds. | 06/until-staged-source |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L419:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L419) | While the **attribution engine** is enabled it reports the same finding from a much lower threshold — `enable({ fanOut })`, default 250 — on the root invalidations it stamps (a signal or store write, a `refresh()`, an async landing), counting the live subscriber list itself on the write, and adds `data.write: "write" \| "refresh" \| "async"` naming the invalidation; where the per-scope warnings below blame the _reader_, this is the fan-out actually happening, priced at the moment it happens. | 08/cost-fanout-engine-handover; 08/cost-fanout-invalidation-kinds; findings/025-async-fanout-classification/repro.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L419:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L419) | The engine hands over to the core at 2000, so one change never carries two findings. | 08/cost-fanout-core-threshold; 08/cost-fanout-engine-handover; 08/cost-checks-false |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L433:6](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L433) | `data`: `route`, `grew` (the series that climbed), `history` (the `GraphSize` at each settle, oldest first), `roots`, `routes`, `interaction`. | 08/cost-graph-growth-default; 08/cost-graph-growth-routes-subject |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L445:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L445) | - `WIDE_SCOPE_DEPS`: a scope's dependency count reached 200 (re-warns after another 50% growth), with the source names listed. | 08/cost-wide-default-threshold; 08/cost-wide-growth-default |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L461:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L461) | An async flight (a promise or async iterable entering the system) formed a sequential chain behind an upstream flight. | 08/attribution-warning-async_waterfall |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L700:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L700) | The one shape it cannot see is a bare identifier bound to a function — `const renderHead = () => props.header; <div>{renderHead}</div>` — which is a value to the compiler and a thunk to the runtime. | tracks/docs/unscoped.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L700:5](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L700) | Unscoped, its content takes ids from the parent's counter where each runtime happens to evaluate it: the client's `insert` at the statement, the server's `ssr()` inside the walk after every scoped sibling reserved its slot — the keys permute, the client builds detached copies, handlers land on nodes the user cannot see. | tracks/docs/unscoped.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L700:6](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L700) | `JSX.Element` excludes functions in 2.0, so type-checked code cannot write this hole; by ruling it is **not** scoped (no production cost for a shape the types reject), and this check is what catches it — from JavaScript or through a cast. | tracks/docs/diagnostic-types.tsx; tracks/docs/compiler-addressability.test.ts; tracks/docs/unscoped.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L702:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L702) | Unscoped allocation alone is not the finding: a function hole with nothing scoped after it in its template lands on the same ids on both sides and stays silent. (An `<Errored>` zero-arity `fallback={() => <Fallback />}` thunk used to be the common instance — handed back unresolved and built by the consuming hole; `<Errored>` now calls a function-valued fallback inside its own scope whatever its arity, so that shape never reaches a hole.) Each side reports the permutation it can see. | tracks/docs/unscoped.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L702:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L702) | The server records the counter's next id when the hole was registered (`data.registered`, argument evaluation — where the client builds it) and around its evaluation in the walk (`data.before` → `data.after`), and reports when the hole allocated at a shifted position; `data.hole` is the hole's position in its template. | tracks/docs/unscoped.test.ts |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L801:7](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L801) | Listeners are observers: a throwing listener is reported through `console.error` and the call, the render, the stream and the other listeners are unaffected; nothing a listener does reaches the result. | 08/records-outlive-engine; tracks/records/records.test.ts: listener-throw; tracks/frames/server-hooks.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L801:9](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L801) | A subscription is the channel's, not any emitter's: it outlives the attribution engine's `enable()`/`disable()` cycles and is dropped only by the function `subscribe` returned. | 08/attribution-holds; 08/records-listener-lifecycle |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L803:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L803) | The types layer the way the packages do, each augmenting only the one beneath it: `@solidjs/signals` declares `RecordTypes`, extending `HostRecordTypes`, with the attribution engine's ten entries on it directly — the engine ships in that package, behind its own entry — `rerun`, `create`, `effect`, `flush`, `flight`, `fallback`, `interaction`, `hold`, `navigation`, `graph`; `solid-js` augments `RecordTypes` with its records (`"boundary"`, `"recovery"`); `@solidjs/web` augments `HostRecordTypes`, through `declare module "solid-js"`, with what it emits (`"invocation"`, `"render"`, `"call"`, `"request"`, `"frame"`). | tracks/docs/types-positive.tsx: 17 record subscriptions and ServerTrace provider merge; tracks/docs/types-negative.tsx: forbidden fields and callback types |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L803:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L803) | So `OBSERVE.records.subscribe(…)` types with the engine's records and every loaded runtime's from a single `solid-js` import, and each interface has exactly one augmenter (TypeScript merges an augmentation onto the declaration its alias resolves to; two packages augmenting one interface through different aliases would not both land). | tracks/docs/types-positive.tsx: 17 record subscriptions and ServerTrace provider merge; tracks/docs/types-negative.tsx: forbidden fields and callback types |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L803:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L803) | Seven runtime records so far, beside the engine's ten (described under [Run attribution](#run-attribution--why-did-this-run); none is emitted until `attribution.enable()`). | tracks/records/trace.test.ts; 08/attribution-holds; 08/records-listener-lifecycle |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L803:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L803) | `OBSERVE.records.observed(type)` is the one gate an emitter of either kind consults before building a record. | tracks/records/trace.test.ts; 08/attribution-holds; 08/records-listener-lifecycle |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L816:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L816) | A boundary whose content rendered on its first pass emits nothing — there was no wait to attribute, the same rule as the client's `hold` records. | 08/boundary-render-sync-silent |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L816:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L816) | For one that waited: `id` is the boundary's hydration id, the id the `SSR_RENDER_ERROR_CONTAINED` finding names in `data.boundary`, so a record and a finding pair by it; `at` is `performance.now()` at discovery; `durationMs` runs discovery → settle — to the content being complete, or to the decision that the server will not produce it; `passes` counts render passes over the content (the discovery pass plus one per wait — `2` is one round of async, more is a sequential chain, a read that depended on the answer to the previous one); `streamed` says whether the outcome reached the client after the shell had flushed (the user saw the fallback, then the swap) or in time to inline into it. | 08/boundary-render-one-wait; 08/boundary-render-error-original; 08/boundary-render-shell-wait |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L816:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L816) | `outcome` is how it ended: `"settled"` — rendered on the server and swapped in; `"fallback"` — the renderer had no stream to settle into (`renderToString`), the fallback shipped final and the client renders the content; `"client"` — the content is client-only (`ssrSource: "client"`); `"error"` — the content threw, `live.error` is the value as thrown, and the paired finding says where it went. | 08/boundary-render-one-wait; 08/boundary-render-string-fallback; 08/boundary-render-client-outcome; 08/boundary-render-error-original |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L816:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L816) | Under a `<Reveal>` group the record names the group and waits for the group's reveal, so `heldMs` is real: how long the finished content sat behind its siblings (`order="together"`, a sequential tail) — `0` for every boundary whose swap was issued as it settled. | 08/boundary-render-reveal-held; 08/boundary-render-one-wait |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L816:5](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L816) | `ownerPath` locates the boundary by component, the same labels the diagnostics carry. | 08/boundary-render-explicit-component-labels |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L816:6](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L816) | The cost is paid only with a listener installed (or in dev, where the checks below read the same facts): a boundary with none takes no clock readings. | 08/boundary-render-unobserved-clocks |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L818:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L818) | Two dev checks are derived from these facts, so the console and a test's `expectNoDiagnostics` see what an agent would otherwise have to read off the record: `SSR_BOUNDARY_WATERFALL` when `passes - 1` sequential waits reach two, and `SSR_CLIENT_CONTENT_MASKED` when a client-only outcome surfaced only after a wait. | 08/diagnostic-server-boundary-waterfall; 08/diagnostic-server-client-content-masked |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L855:10](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L855) | It is absent when the call failed before the request was built (argument serialization threw), when the address is relative and there is no `location` to resolve it against (absent beats a URL that was never sent), and when the reconstruction itself fails (an init the `Request` constructor rejects but the configured `fetch` tolerates — an invalid header name, say): nothing taken for the record may fail the call, and nothing is logged. | tracks/records/records.test.ts: serialization-before-fetch, no-location, invalid-request/header reconstruction, response clone-refused, SSE and generator handoff |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L855:11](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L855) | `live.response` is then a `clone()` taken as the response arrived, before the transport's decode, so its body is whole and unread while the caller still gets its result from the original — except where the clone would be a branch nobody drains, where it is the transport's own object: an event-stream response (a `live()` source, `text/event-stream` — a connection that stays open for the page's life, whose clone would buffer every event ever sent), a deferred result (`deferred: true` — a generator's stream, served for the stream's life; its clone, taken before the result's shape was known, is cancelled at settle so its branch stops buffering), and a response the `clone()` refused (one a configured `fetch` handed over already read, claimed by the `responseHandler`). | tracks/records/records.test.ts: serialization-before-fetch, no-location, invalid-request/header reconstruction, response clone-refused, SSE and generator handoff |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L855:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L855) | Whether bodies are taken is read once, as the call starts (`records.observed("call", "bodies")` — and, for the request alone, `records.observed("request", "bodies")`, see the `"request"` record below), from the union of the listeners installed — so a listener that did not ask, sharing a call with one that did, receives the same `live` as it does, the clone included. | tracks/records/records.test.ts: GET/POST/status/prepare/body-shapes/pending-row/late-listener/duplicate-listener/origin-async-prepare/after-await |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L855:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L855) | The `live` is **one object per call**, across its records: the `CallLive` the `"call"` listener receives at settle is, by identity, the one the call's `"request"` listener received at the send, filled in as the call proceeded (`args` from the start, `request` at the send, `response` and `result`/`error` at settle — filled on **every** settle, whether or not a `"call"` record goes out, so a `"request"` listener holding it reads the outcome off it too) — so an in-process consumer joins the two the way `origin` joins a record to the interaction's, by identity, with no id-plus-time match and no sequence number. | tracks/records/records.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L855:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L855) | It is one **mutable** object shared by both records' listeners: treat it as read-only — a write shows up on the other record's listener. | tracks/records/records.test.ts: streamCase transport handoff, intercept, shared-request, mixed-bodies, late-request/late-call and slow-listener exact clocks |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L855:5](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L855) | The same goes for `live.request`: one `Request` for the call, shared by the two records, and a body reads once — a listener that wants the body reads it through `live.request.clone()` (`live.request.clone().text()`) so the other record's listener still can; a direct `live.request.text()` leaves `bodyUsed` true for whoever reads next. | tracks/records/records.test.ts: streamCase transport handoff, intercept, shared-request, mixed-bodies, late-request/late-call and slow-listener exact clocks |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L855:7](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L855) | With no such listener `live.request` is absent and `live.response` is the transport's own object — status and headers readable, body consumed by its decode. | tracks/records/records.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L855:8](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L855) | With the opt-in, `live.request` is the request as dispatched — the final url and `RequestInit`, transport headers and the `prepareRequest` hook applied — built into a `Request` of the listener's own at the send, so its headers and body are readable in full without touching what the transport sent. | tracks/records/records.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L855:9](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L855) | It carries the body only when the body has a shape a second `Request` can hold without a competing consumer — a `string`, `URLSearchParams`, `FormData`, `Blob`, `ArrayBuffer` or a view of one — and is built without it otherwise (a `ReadableStream` or an async iterable, the transport's streaming-upload contract: reconstructing one would consume it ahead of the send). | tracks/records/records.test.ts: GET/POST/status/prepare/body-shapes/pending-row/late-listener/duplicate-listener/origin-async-prepare/after-await |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L869:10](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L869) | A reconstruction that failed leaves `live.request` absent and the record is delivered regardless — the request was handed over either way. | tracks/records/records.test.ts: GET/POST/status/prepare/body-shapes/pending-row/late-listener/duplicate-listener/origin-async-prepare/after-await |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L869:12](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L869) | A call whose fetch never settles emits `"request"` alone; a deferred or streaming result emits `"request"` at the send and `"call"` at handoff. | tracks/records/records.test.ts: GET/POST/status/prepare/body-shapes/pending-row/late-listener/duplicate-listener/origin-async-prepare/after-await |

"Left" means handed over, not that it reached the network: a `fetch` that throws **synchronously** still has its `"request"`, and a `"call"` with `outcome: "error"` behind it. | tracks/records/records.test.ts: GET/POST/status/prepare/body-shapes/pending-row/late-listener/duplicate-listener/origin-async-prepare/after-await |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L923:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L923) | The runtime derives a request's trace itself in every tier — the W3C `traceparent` half is core HTTP behavior, see `getTraceContext()` in [RFC 12](12-ssr-http.md#the-trace-the-request-belongs-to-gettracecontext) — and this slot is how an observer overrides or extends that derivation **once, globally**, without a per-request entry point into the host: the provider is asked once per request (or per render), at the first read or at shell flush, and its answer merges over the derivation — fields it returns replace the derived ones, its `entries` merge by name over the runtime's `traceparent`. | tracks/records/trace-cases.tsx; tracks/records/records.test.ts |

| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1121:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1121) | **Records and clocks.** Everything the engine hands out — `RerunEvent`, `InteractionEvent`, `HoldEvent`, `NavigationEvent` — is a record with an absolute `at` on the `performance.now()` clock (`RerunEvent.at` the run's start, `HoldEvent.at` the start of the wait, `NavigationEvent.at`/`InteractionEvent.at` the request/dispatch) plus durations from it (`holdMs`, `settledMs`, `selfMs`). | tracks/docs/attribution-isolation.test.ts: public cause kinds and named source writes; 08/interaction-action-navigation; 08/timeline-hold-navigation-interaction |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1121:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1121) | Epoch time for an exporter is `performance.timeOrigin + at` (milliseconds). | tracks/docs/attribution-isolation.test.ts; findings/012-attribution-audience-gate/repro.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1121:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1121) | Records are serializable as emitted: none carries a live graph reference — a re-run names its scope by `nodeId` (the engine's per-node id, stable across the scope's runs in the process, distinct between scopes; in-process consumers get the node itself as the listener's second argument, `live`) — while the frame objects that join records (`origin`, `interaction`) are the same object across records in-process, so join by identity there and by `ChangeOrigin.run`/`at`/`name` once they have left it. | 08/timeline-hold-navigation-interaction; 08/attribution-records-queries |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1121:5](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1121) | `OBSERVE.records.subscribe(type, listener)` delivers each record synchronously at the moment it is complete (a re-run at recompute end; an interaction, hold or navigation when it settles), bottom-up: a hold before the navigation it held, before the interaction that performed it — the same objects `history(type)` keeps. | 08/timeline-hold-navigation-interaction; 08/attribution-records-queries |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1121:7](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1121) | A `RerunEvent` is built — dep diff, previews, cause list, ring-buffer push — only while something wants it: a `rerun` listener, an imported fold (`costs`/`feedback`) or the console log. | tracks/docs/attribution-isolation.test.ts; findings/012-attribution-audience-gate/repro.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1121:8](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1121) | Without one the engine still keeps its per-node facts and runs every check (hot runs, hot time, wasted recompute, dep width) from them, allocates no record, and `history("rerun")` stays empty. | tracks/docs/attribution-isolation.test.ts; findings/012-attribution-audience-gate/repro.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1123:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1123) | `flush` — one per scheduler drain: `at`, `durationMs`, `runs` and `created` inside it, `held` (a transition parked), `interaction`. | 08/timeline-hold-navigation-interaction; 08/attribution-records-queries |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1123:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1123) | `create` — a computation's creation run (`RerunEvent`'s shape without causes: `nodeId`, `nodeName`, `nodeKind`, `at`, `selfMs`, `totalMs`, `depCount`, `phase`, `held`, `interaction`) — the mount work that no `RerunEvent` describes, and what `InteractionEvent.created` counts. | 08/timeline-hold-navigation-interaction; 08/attribution-records-queries |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1123:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1123) | `effect` — an effect's callback, the imperative half that writes the DOM: `nodeId`, `nodeName`, `at`, `durationMs`, `run`, `interaction`. | 08/timeline-hold-navigation-interaction; 08/attribution-records-queries |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1123:5](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1123) | `flight` — an async node's flight, kickoff to landing: `nodeId`, `nodeName`, `ownerPath`, `at`, `durationMs`, `outcome: "landed" \| "abandoned"` (superseded before it landed), `interaction`. | 08/late-final-flight-fields-and-fold |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1123:6](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1123) | `fallback` — a `<Loading>` boundary's fallback, displayed to hidden: `ownerPath`, `at`, `shownMs`, `interaction`. | 08/late-final-fallback-fields-and-fold |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1123:8](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1123) | Every derived `ChangeRecord` on a re-run's cause chain also carries the `nodeId` of the memo it came from, so a consumer can walk the propagation of one write node by node. | 08/late-final-derived-cause-node-ids |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1125:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1125) | **Joining an interaction to the browser's INP entry.** An `InteractionEvent.at` is the DOM event's `timeStamp` when the runtime dispatched it (compiled event bindings pass it; `OBSERVE.attribution.withInteraction({ …, at: e.timeStamp }, fn)` for a custom dispatcher), and `inputDelayMs` is the queueing from that moment to the handler's entry — the browser's input delay. | 08/timeline-hold-navigation-interaction; tracks/records/interaction.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1125:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1125) | The `PerformanceEventTiming` entry for the same event (a `PerformanceObserver` on `"event"`) has `startTime === interaction.at` on the same clock and carries `interactionId`, so the join is `entry.startTime === interaction.at`: `inputDelayMs` accounts for the entry's `processingStart − startTime`, `handlerMs` for its processing, and `settledMs` extends past its `duration` to when Solid had the screen right. | tracks/records/input.test.ts: native EventTiming startTime/timeStamp/interaction.at, interactionId, inputDelayMs, handlerMs and settledMs |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1127:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1127) | An interaction whose writes all went to excluded subjects, with none of the app's work run — a click on the observer's own panel — is not recorded at all. | 08/excluded-owner-attribution; 08/exclude-include |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1127:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1127) | Mark the root as it is created (a store's nodes take the owner the store was created under, recorded only once the engine is enabled — enable before creating the panel's stores). | 08/excluded-owner-attribution |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1127:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1127) | The signals and stores created under it are excluded subjects wherever their writes come from — a click handler, an adapter callback — so writes need no `runWithOwner`, and must not use one: a write under an owner is a write in an owned scope (`REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`). | 08/excluded-owner-attribution |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1127:5](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1127) | `OBSERVE.isExcluded(subject)` answers the question for any owner or node. | 08/excluded-owner-attribution; 08/exclude-include |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1129:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1129) | `OBSERVE.include(owner)` is the inverse: it marks an owner subtree as the app's, inside an excluded one. | 08/excluded-owner-attribution; 08/exclude-include |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1129:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1129) | The question is answered by the **nearest marked ancestor** — an owner under an included owner under an excluded root is watched, so an observer that renders the app _inside_ its own shell (`<DevToolbar><App /></DevToolbar>` — the toolbar excludes its root, then includes the owner it renders the app's children under) hides its chrome and still sees everything the app does. | 08/excluded-owner-attribution; 08/exclude-include |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1129:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1129) | An owner carries one mark: a later `exclude` or `include` on the same owner replaces it, and — like `exclude` — a mark belongs at the owner's creation: the engine caches each node's verdict the first time it asks (`_devExcluded`, for the node's life), so a mark set or replaced after the subtree has run does not reach nodes already judged. | 08/late-final-exclude-cached-verdicts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1131:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1131) | The engine governs those fields **at the source**, with one option on the hold — `attribution.enable({ values })` — so a record never carries what the level excludes and nothing downstream (a formatter, `@solidjs/web/performance-tracks`, an exporter) has to scrub. | tracks/docs/attribution-isolation.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1133:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1133) | - `"full"` — **the dev default**: previews on every change record and held write; the element text on every target; the sentences quote both. | tracks/docs/attribution-isolation.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1134:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1134) | - `"labels"` — no value previews anywhere (`prev`/`value` absent, sentences say `wrote "page"` and `signal "n" write` without the `1 → 2`); element text kept only on a `button` or an `a` (`button#save "Save"` stays, `div#card "Personal note"` becomes `div#card`) — the control's label, never a cell's content. | tracks/docs/attribution-isolation.test.ts |
| [08-dev-diagnostics:L1135:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/08-dev-diagnostics.md#L1135) | - `"none"` — **the observe default**: no previews, no element text on any target (`button#save`); the sentences name the node and the element and nothing the person typed or read. | tracks/docs/attribution-isolation.test.ts |

| [10-server-functions:L36:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L36) | This works because the directive marks the function _by position_ — the compiler swaps exactly the expression carrying the directive and touches nothing around it. | tracks/frames/compiler.test.ts:function-directive |
| [10-server-functions:L37:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L37) | **Anything referenced only inside a `"use server"` body never reaches the client.** The extraction replaces the body with a reference, and the directive pass’s orphan-scoped dead-code elimination removes now-unused imports and bindings — schema libraries, database handles, helper imports all vanish from client output. | tracks/frames/compiler.test.ts:function-directive |
| [10-server-functions:L37:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L37) | **The directive boundary is itself the privacy mechanism.** | tracks/frames/compiler.test.ts:function-directive |
| [10-server-functions:L39:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L39) | One architectural fact worth stating, because the two directive levels land on opposite sides of it: **where a wrapper sits relative to the directive decides what it wraps.** At function level the directive marks the inner function, so `registerServerReference(id, fn)` registers the raw function for HTTP dispatch before any wrapper runs — wrapper-position code (`GET(...)` around a `"use server"` body) affects only the client transport and the in-process callable, never HTTP dispatch. | tracks/frames/compiler.test.ts |
| [10-server-functions:L39:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L39) | That is why the function-level declaration surface (`GET`) is transport-only, and why dispatch-path concerns there belong inside the body. | tracks/frames/compiler.test.ts |
| [10-server-functions:L41:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L41) | **Module-level directives invert this: the export’s _evaluated value_ is the server function.** `export const getUser = withValidation(schema, fn)` in a module-level `"use server"` file registers the wrapper’s _return value_ — the composed function — so server-side wrappers (validation, auth, logging, `withDelay(fn, 400)` mock latency) apply to **every call path**: HTTP dispatch and in-process SSR calls alike. | tracks/frames/compiler.test.ts |
| [10-server-functions:L41:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L41) | Nothing here contradicts the function-level fact; the directive just sits on the other side of the wrapper, so the wrapper is _inside_ the registration instead of outside it. | tracks/frames/compiler.test.ts |
| [10-server-functions:L41:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L41) | First, wrappers are server-only by construction — the client build is rebuilt from scratch as bare reference exports (`createServerReference(id)`), so wrapper code, schemas, and helpers never ship. | tracks/frames/compiler.test.ts:module-directive |
| [10-server-functions:L41:5](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L41) | Second, the compiler stays out of the pattern-matching business: it never asks “which argument is the function” (the `server$()` mistake the positional directive was adopted to end) — it registers the terminal initializer whole, whatever expression it is. | tracks/frames/compiler.test.ts:module-directive |
| [10-server-functions:L41:6](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L41) | Third, the shape check moves to the runtime: `registerServerReference` throws at module eval when handed a non-function, so a stray `export const limit = 5` in a directive module fails the server boot loudly instead of shipping a dead reference the client discovers per-call. | 10/reference-nonfunction |
| [10-server-functions:L41:7](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L41) | Aliasing composes freely (`async function f() {...}` … `export { f }`; alias chains; default exports, named or anonymous — the compiler synthesizes a binding for anonymous default expressions): the alias trace ends at the terminal initializer and registers that. | tracks/frames/compiler.test.ts:module-directive |

| [10-server-functions:L47:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L47) | **Client:** `configureServerFunctionsClient({ endpoint?, codec?, fetch?, prepareRequest?, serializeArgs?, responseHandler? })` — call once in the client entry, only when deviating from the defaults (endpoint defaults to `/_server`; `codec` takes seroval plugin options and must match the server’s; `fetch` replaces the function the transport sends with — always called as `(address, init)` — for concerns the runtime has no opinion about: retries, telemetry, a test double, or pointing calls at a route of the app’s own, which the handler serves through the same `Request` it serves everything else with; `prepareRequest` is the transport middleware hook below; `responseHandler` is the integration seam server components install — see [RFC 11](11-server-components.md)). | tracks/frames/transport-contracts.test.ts; tracks/frames/client-codec.test.ts; tracks/frames/live-scopes.test.ts; tracks/frames/live-loop.test.ts; tracks/frames/frames.test.ts |
| [10-server-functions:L47:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L47) | Compiled client output produces callables that POST to the call’s **data address** — `<endpoint>/data/<id>`, the id in a path segment — with no per-call header of their own (a read is exactly its url to caches and `<link rel="preload">`). | tracks/frames/frames.test.ts:client-rpc |
| [10-server-functions:L47:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L47) | The data address is the scripted transport’s own path, where answers are the codec’s; the bare `<endpoint>/<id>` address (a reference’s `.url`, what renders into form actions) answers plain HTTP. | tracks/frames/frames.test.ts:rpc |
| [10-server-functions:L47:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L47) | Two paths because the two caller kinds get differently shaped answers and shared caches key on the URL: with one shape per path, a cached answer can only ever be replayed to the caller kind it was made for. (A third caller kind, `live`'s loop, gets a third path — `<endpoint>/live/<id>`, answered as an event stream; same rule.) **Argument encoding (updated since first draft):** arguments with a natural HTTP encoding (a lone string, FormData, File, Blob, ...) go as-is; everything else is sent as **plain JSON by default** — no serializer in the client bundle — and values JSON can’t carry faithfully (Dates, Maps, Sets, typed arrays, cycles, a top-level `undefined` — except in a bound call whose trailing argument is a body-like object (FormData, URLSearchParams, File, Blob, ...), where the leading arguments ride `?args=` and `undefined` is sent as `null` as in the action url; a trailing string is not a body in this sense, so `fn(1, undefined, "str")` goes through the codec) **throw with a directed message** unless you opt in once via `enableRichArguments()` from `@solidjs/web/server-functions/rich-args`, which installs the codec’s write half (~5 KB gz) as `serializeArgs` — importing the entry is the opt-in at the module-graph level, so the serializer ships only when the app asks for it. _Results_ are unaffected — they always travel through the codec, whose decode half the client carries regardless. | tracks/frames/frames.test.ts:plain/native/rich arguments and results; findings/024-single-typed-array-encoding/repro.test.ts |
| [10-server-functions:L47:5](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L47) | Async returns (promises, streams) settle over the open connection via length-prefixed chunk framing; a `live` source's response — requested at the live address — is the same stream framed as server-sent events (see `live(fn)` under the extension surface). (A `@solidjs/web/serialization` subpath exists; most of it is integration-facing plumbing — the bridge exposing the runtime’s serializer machinery for the runtime’s own entries and for integrations building transports — exempt from the 2.0 stability guarantee and subject to change. | 10/live-wire-framing |
| [10-server-functions:L47:6](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L47) | The one application-facing part is plugin _authoring_: `createPlugin` and `OpaqueReference` are re-exported there from the runtime’s own seroval instance, and custom plugins for the `codec` option must be built from that import — a plugin built against your own `seroval` dependency edge would not fail the build, it would emit nodes the other end of the wire can’t interpret (the version-pinning lesson of solid-start #1474). | 10/codec-custom-public-edge; tracks/frames/codec-edge.test.ts#independent foreign instance rejects / runtime public instance succeeds |

| [10-server-functions:L63:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L63) | The `request` handed here is a **standards-shaped `Request` and nothing more** — url, method, headers, signal, readable body. | 10/request-standard-shape |

| [10-server-functions:L63:5](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L63) | The host has its own request in closure when it calls the handler — read platform handles there and put them on the event (`locals`), which is where integrations should surface them regardless. | 10/request-standard-shape |

| [10-server-functions:L230:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/10-server-functions.md#L230) | Which framing a call gets is decided the way the runtime decides every answer shape — by the **address**: the loop requests `<endpoint>/live/<id>`, a sibling of the scripted `/data/<id>` address, and the server frames whatever it answers there as an event stream. | 10/live-wire-framing |

(Left pending, it held every write of the tick that released it.) | [12-ssr-http:C230:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L230) | // deleting = expiring: empty value + Max-Age=0, matching the path/domain it was set under | 12/cookie-delete |

| [12-ssr-http:L237:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L237) | - **Both entries export the one real implementation.** A pure value transformer has legitimate browser uses (`document.cookie = serializeCookie(...)`, parsing `document.cookie`), and a client-side no-op stub would hand back silent garbage — so isomorphic code like a shared render path can call it anywhere. | 12/cookie-roundtrip |
| [12-ssr-http:L237:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L237) | Nothing in the client runtime imports it internally, so it tree-shakes out of bundles that don’t. | tracks/docs/cookie-bundle.test.ts |
| [12-ssr-http:L238:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L238) | - **Reads are a request-only view.** The `Cookie` header is what the client sent; an appended `Set-Cookie` in the same request does **not** read back — the request is what arrived, the response is what you’re building, and merging the two invents a browser round trip that hasn’t happened. | 12/cookie-response-not-request |
| [12-ssr-http:L239:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L239) | - **Writes are event-time mutations of the outgoing head** — `Headers.append` semantics exactly, owning no scope and never retracting. (Cookies declared through `httpHeader` are also simply correct now — its retraction snapshots and restores `set-cookie` entry-exactly instead of comma-joining — but the append above is the blessed spelling.) | 12/cookie-scope-independent; 12/cookie-declaration-retraction |
| [12-ssr-http:L240:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L240) | - **Committed is a hard line, never a silent one — enforced on the stub itself.** A late cookie is imperative data (a session being established); losing it is a bug. | 12/committed-header-mutation |
| [12-ssr-http:L240:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L240) | The moment the head freezes (shell flush, or the server-function commit seam), the stub’s `headers` mutating methods fail loudly: a post-commit write **throws in the dev build** and reports through `console.error` (and no-ops) in production, where crashing a request that is already streaming would compound the bug. | 12/committed-header-mutation |
| [12-ssr-http:L240:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L240) | Because the enforcement lives on the stub, it covers every writer uniformly — direct appends, middleware, anything — not just code polite enough to check `committed` first. | 12/committed-header-mutation |
| [12-ssr-http:L242:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L242) | **The multi-`Set-Cookie` guarantee.** `Set-Cookie` is the one header that must never fold: multiple values are separate headers, commas are legal _inside_ a single value (`Expires`), and `Headers` iteration semantics differ across runtimes. | 12/ssr-response-cookies; 12/commit-cookies |
| [12-ssr-http:L242:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L242) | Every place core materializes a response head — `createSSRResponse`’s derivation (including its redirect paths), the server-function handler’s response encoding and forwarded `respond()`/`redirect()` metadata, the no-JS form redirect — carries `Set-Cookie` values entry-by-entry via `getSetCookie()` + append, never `get`/`set` or constructor-copy folding. | 12/ssr-response-cookies; 12/commit-cookies |
| [12-ssr-http:C260:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L260) | const MAX_AGE = 60 * 60 * 24 * 7; // seconds — drives the cookie AND the enforced expiry | tracks/docs/session-recipe.test.ts |

| [12-ssr-http:C264:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L264) | // secret and keep old ones until outstanding cookies expire. | tracks/docs/session-recipe.test.ts |
| [12-ssr-http:C266:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L266) | httpOnly: true, // set explicitly — the package defaults httpOnly/secure to false | tracks/docs/session-recipe.test.ts |

| [12-ssr-http:L299:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L299) | - **Signed, not encrypted.** HMAC-SHA256 keeps the payload tamper-proof; it stays client-readable, so nothing secret goes in it. | tracks/docs/session-recipe.test.ts |

| [12-ssr-http:L301:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L301) | - **Expiry is enforced server-side.** The signed format itself carries no expiry (the MAC covers the value only), so the recipe embeds `exp` in the payload and checks it on read. | tracks/docs/session-recipe.test.ts |

| [12-ssr-http:L308:3](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L308) | A handler's response leaves through one of exactly **two exits**: page results through `createSSRResponse`, any other `Response` — a middleware early return, an API result — through `commitEventResponse`. | 12/ssr-response-string; 12/stream-response-head; 12/commit-other-status |

| [12-ssr-http:L327:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L327) | - **At shell flush** — the moment the head freezes — the stub is `committed` and its status/headers are merged over `options.responseInit` (`Set-Cookie` values survive as separate entries; `Server-Timing` folds entry by entry; `content-type` defaults to `text/html; charset=utf-8`). | 12/ssr-head-defaults; 12/ssr-response-cookies; 12/trace-sampled-wire |
| [12-ssr-http:L327:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L327) | The commit is also where the request's trace reaches the head (`Server-Timing: traceparent;desc="…"` — see `getTraceContext()`). | 12/trace-sampled-wire |
| [12-ssr-http:L328:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L328) | - **A `Location` present before the flush** becomes a real redirect instead of an HTML response: bodyless, carrying the stub’s cookies, with the status from `getExpectedRedirectStatus` (also exported — the stub’s own status when it is a redirect status, `302` otherwise, because a status set for the page render doesn’t describe the redirect that preempts it). | 12/early-redirect |
| [12-ssr-http:L329:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L329) | - **A `Location` set after the flush** can only be honored client-side: stream completion appends `<script>window.location=…</script>` before closing, carrying `options.nonce` so a strict `script-src` CSP doesn’t block it. | 12/late-redirect-script |
| [12-ssr-http:L330:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L330) | - **A render that ends before the flush** (it failed, which `onError` hears as `handling: "failed"`, or its `signal` aborted) produced no page: the promise resolves with a bodyless `500` (a `Location` already on the stub still redirects) and the stub is committed. | 12/aborted-before-shell |
| [12-ssr-http:L330:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L330) | It never rejects. | 12/aborted-before-shell |

| [12-ssr-http:L333:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L333) | String results return a `Response` synchronously; stream results return a promise that resolves at shell flush, so returning it from a fetch handler sends the head at the right moment by construction. | 12/ssr-response-string; 12/stream-response-head |
| [12-ssr-http:L335:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L335) | - `commitEventResponse(response, event?)` is the **other exit** — handler-lifecycle plumbing for a `Response` that did not go through `createSSRResponse` (a middleware early return, an API result), the same fold the server-function handler's own responses take. | 12/commit-cookies; 12/commit-other-precedence; 12/commit-other-status |
| [12-ssr-http:L335:2](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L335) | It folds the event's stub onto the response — `Set-Cookie` appends entry-by-entry alongside the response's own, `Server-Timing` folds by name beside the response's own metrics, other stub headers fill gaps only (never the wire-protocol family the handlers own, never `Content-Type`/`Content-Length` on a bodiless response), the status is never taken from the stub — then commits the stub, so later writes fail loudly. | 12/commit-cookies; 12/commit-other-precedence; 12/commit-other-status |
| [12-ssr-http:L335:4](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L335) | `event` defaults to the ambient `getRequestEvent()`. | 12/ambient-commit-event |

| [12-ssr-http:L352:1](https://github.com/solidjs/solid/blob/53ef0e69ea78bd6c7d88d5b82db2a13b8b85d712/documentation/solid-2.0/12-ssr-http.md#L352) | `next()` advances the chain (an optional `Request` argument substitutes the request downstream); the terminal `next` handed to the composed function dispatches to the actual handler. | 12/middleware-order; 12/middleware-request |
